# THE BOOK OF METEORS BY THEOPHRASTUS
## German Philosopher of Hohenheim, on Meteorological Phenomena from the Element of Fire or the Heavens, and their impressions.

When God alone willed to create the Earth and, in His divine wisdom, pre-considered how and in what manner it should be, He divided it into four parts. Thus, there are four <term>bodies <note>original: Corpora; here referring to the four fundamental elements</note></term>. These bodies were to be the "mothers" of all things that would belong to the one whom He would create in His own image—that is, Adam, which is Mankind. Once this purpose was decided within God, the four bodies were created: namely, Heaven, Earth, Water, and Air. For as the <term>Scripture <note>referring to the Book of Genesis in the Bible</note></term> says, He created the Heavens first, then the Earth, followed by the others of their kind.

<summary>This page serves as the introduction to Paracelsus’s work on meteorology, explaining that God created the four elements (Heaven, Earth, Water, and Air) as fundamental "mothers" to sustain Mankind.</summary>
<keywords>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, Paracelsus, Meteorica, element of fire, four bodies, Adam, four elements, creation, Genesis</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to Paracelsus’s cosmology. It expands on the relationship between the four fundamental "bodies" (elements) and the human being, specifically how these elements provide nourishment. The catchword "Complexio-" at the bottom right indicates the next page will discuss the four "complexions" or humoral temperaments of man.</meta>

->The Book<-

Now know at this time that the four <term>bodies <note>original: Corpora; Paracelsus uses this to refer to the four physical elements—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire—as containers of life.</note></term> are the only "mothers." They are called this because they bear fruits and assist them, so that Mankind may be nourished by them. For example: the Earth bears its fruit; Mankind is nourished by that fruit, but not by the Earth itself. In the same way, Heaven is a distinct body in its own right, from which fruits alone go out to sustain Mankind. How Heaven shows and gives its fruit, I will present in this book. I have indicated this <term>chapter <note>original: Caput</note></term> solely to show that Heaven is a body for itself, independent of all others, and it gives its fruit to Mankind just as the Earth does.

---

->CONCERNING THE FATHER OF THE ELEMENTS,<-
->and the Mother of the Elements.<-
->Chapter 1.<-
<note>original Latin: DE PATRE ELEMENTORUM, & matre Elementorum. Cap. 1.</note>

Now know further what has been said regarding the four elements: how there are four elements, and how Mankind must have them, and that they are divided into the four complexio-

### ON METEORS <note>original Latin: METEORORVM.</note>

<term>Complexions <note>In medieval science, a "complexion" refers to the specific balance of the four qualities—hot, cold, moist, and dry—that determines the nature of a substance or person.</note></term>: However, it should not be as the ancients have written—namely, that the Earth is cold and dry. That is not so. Rather, in some places it is cold and dry, in others cold and moist, in others hot and moist, and also hot and dry. But it is an element; that is, she is a "Mother" of fruits. And it is an element because it is a Mother of the things that sustain Mankind, and not because of its complexion.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;So too with Water; it is said to be cold and moist, yet it is often hot and dry, and cold and dry, etc. For Water is not created so that it must be cold and moist, but rather it must be wet; nothing else matters. This wetness, however, is often hot and dry in its internal power, yet the <term>body <note>original: Corpus</note></term> is wet in its physical nature. The earth is dry so that the fruit may proceed out of it and within it in its proper course.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Similarly, Heaven is not of one complexion, but of many. It is not a fire, though it is considered a fire. It is "the fire" in this way: a fire comes from it, but sometimes water comes from it, sometimes fire, sometimes cold, sometimes warm, etc. This much you should know and understand: that the elements are solely Mothers, and are not to be forced into a single complexion, but—

A ij
<meta>catchword: —rather in</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus rejects the classical Greek definitions of the elements, arguing that Earth, Water, and Heaven (Fire) do not have fixed qualities like "cold" or "dry," but instead function as versatile "Mothers" that produce whatever is necessary for life.</summary>
<keywords>elements, complexions, Paracelsus, Earth, Water, Heaven, qualities, four humors, cosmology, Mother of fruits</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the four elements as "Mothers" or generative sources. Paracelsus is explaining that the physical properties of an element (like the hardness of Earth or the wetness of Water) are designed specifically to support the types of life and matter that originate within them.</meta>

### THE BOOK <note>original: Das Buch</note>

—rather in many ways: For just as that which grows from it is, so too is that which made and bore it; as *Spearwort* <note>original Latin: *Flamula*; a yellow flowering plant often associated with a "biting" or "flaming" nature.</note> comes from *Spearwort*, and *Nightshade* <note>original Latin: *Solatrum*</note> comes from *Nightshade*.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Now mark this: the Earth is meant to carry mankind and be a dwelling; likewise for stones, cliffs, sand, and all plants. From this it follows then that she must be an element that is **solid** <note>original Latin: *compactum*</note> and firm, so that she may carry such things. From this it follows then that she is hard, for she must be tilled with the plow. Now, water must be wet for the reason that fish must swim in it, which cannot happen in the Earth. And it shall provide salt; it shall provide stone. Now, all such things must be wet in their **first matter** <note>original: *materia*; the raw, unformed state of a substance</note>, and from that wetness they reach a state of **solidification** <note>original Latin: *coagulation*</note>. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;But whatever comes out of the Earth has a seed, a **body** <note>original Latin: *corpus*</note> that is dry, such as seeds, roots, and trees; and all are made of dry **first matter** <note>original Latin: *materia prima*</note> which is **solid** <note>original: *compact*</note>. In water, however, nothing exists in this way in its **first matter**—as a **solid body**—but rather everything is a **fluid** <note>original Latin: *liquor*</note>; this then has its mother from the element of water. And in the element of water grow the **final forms** <note>original Latin: *vltimæ materiæ*; the completed, physical state of a thing</note>, whose beginning is **liquid** <note>original Latin: *liquidum*</note> and **moist** <note>original Latin: *humidum*</note>.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Now the third **body** <note>original Latin: *corpus*</note> is the Air. Now

<meta>catchword: the same</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the physical qualities of Earth and Water are predetermined by the needs of the things they "mother." Earth is solid to support life and structures, while Water is liquid to allow for fish and the "coagulation" of salts and stones.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, elements, materia prima, coagulation, botany, cosmology, four elements, earth, water, air</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the four elements as generative "Mothers." While the previous page focused on the solid nature of Earth and the fluid nature of Water, this page moves upward to define the subtle substances of Air and the Heavens.</meta>

### ->OF METEORS<- <note>original Latin: *Meteororum*; in the 16th century, "meteors" referred to all atmospheric phenomena, including wind, rain, and lightning.</note>

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;That same element [Air] must also have a **body** <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note>, such that this same **body** is not **moist** <note>original Latin: *Humidum*</note> like water, nor solid like the earth. For this reason: all things that grow out of the element have their entrance into the **body of the air** <note>original Latin: *Corpus Aeris*</note>, just as a fish is in the water. Mankind is placed into the **airy body** <note>original Latin: *Corpus Aereum*</note> to walk within it, just as the fish is in the water; likewise also all trees and whatever grows there. Therefore it is now necessary that **Air** <note>original Latin: *Aer*</note> be a **void** <term>original Latin: *Chaos*; for Paracelsus, "Chaos" is the technical name for the element of Air, serving as the space-filling medium between heaven and earth</term>, not an earth, not a water, but rather **transparent** <note>original Latin: *Perspicuum*</note> and **translucent** <note>original Latin: *Diaphanum*</note>—intangible and invisible—so that tangible things may exist within it, and that which is visible may be seen through it; and it is like a glass.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Thus know further that the Heaven likewise is also a **body** <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note> that is not **solid** <note>original Latin: *Compact*</note>—not firm like the earth, not wet like the water, not **transparent** <note>original Latin: *Perspicuum*</note> like the air. Rather, it has a particular body with another essence, such that the Heaven is not **terrestrial** <note>original Latin: *Terrestrisch*</note>; and yet it is a **solid** essence, though not an earthly solidity. It is thin and permeable like water, and yet no water; it is clear and transparent, and yet not like the air. Rather, it is a **body** like a smoke, which distinguishes itself in its essence and **corporeality** <note>original Latin: *Corporalitet*</note> from all others.

<sig>A iij</sig>
<meta>catchword: Bodies [Corporibus].</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus defines the element of Air as a transparent "Chaos" or medium that supports life, and the Heavens as a unique, smoke-like substance distinct from the other three elements.</summary>
<keywords>Air, Aer, Chaos, Heaven, transparency, elements, atmosphere, corporeality, Paracelsus</keywords>

# THE BOOK

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;...**bodies** <note>original Latin: *Corporibus*</note>. That is, it is not like a stone or wood or earth; nor is it like a water, nor like the air. Rather, it is a **Body** <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note> that has no mixture at all with the others. So too the heaven is a **Body** that can best be understood through the nature of **smoke** <note>original German: *Rauch*</note> alone. For it must be so, for this reason: whatever grows from it is also like it—they are **bodies** and yet not bodies; they are **solid** <note>original German: *Compact*</note> and yet not solid; they are **permeable** <note>original German: *durchgreiffig*</note> like water and yet they are not water; they are intangible like the air, yet they are not the air. Such is the Sun, which is a **Body** as has been described, and the Moon and all the stars.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Now, it is without **complexion** <term>original: *Complexion*; in 16th-century natural philosophy, this refers to the specific balance of the four qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) or humors</term>, and it is the **element of fire**, and a **Mother** from which the fire grows and originates. Just as fire has a **corporeality** <note>original: *Corporalitet*</note> about it, so too does the heaven; so also do the stars, which take their nature and essence from the heaven. Therefore, know now of the Heaven: that this same **Body** is a distinct part and an **element of fire**; and whatever fire is meant to do, the Heaven does, for the Heaven gives the fire and the fire grows from it.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Furthermore, know also of the **color of the Heaven**. The Earth has a color that is black through and through, and whatever other color appears within it is a **mineral** <note>original: *Miner*</note>. Water also has a color through...

# OF METEORS
<note>original Latin: *METEORORVM*</note>

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;...and by whatever other colors there are, it is also a **nature** <note>original German: *Miner*; likely referring to the fundamental source or "mine" of a quality</note>. Now, the color of water has no name; it is not white, it is not gray, nor blue, nor green, and yet it may be counted among them all, or perceived as something like them. The Earth also has a color; it is not black, it is not brown, etc., and yet it is something similar to them. So it is also with the Air; there one sees a clear color, transparent in the **Chaos** <term>original: *Chaos*; in early modern physics, this refers to the atmospheric space or "gas" between the heavens and the earth</term>. It is not white, not brown, not yellow, etc., and yet it is something like these colors.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In this same way, the Heaven is also colored; it is at once green, at once blue, at once brown, etc., and yet it is none of those colors, though it may be perceived as such. For such are the **Elemental Bodies**, that no **perfect** <note>meaning "pure" or "singular" in this context</note> color can be called by a single name. But whatever comes *out* of them, that has its own distinct, understandable, and named colors. Since the elements themselves do not have perfect colors, but are as has been stated: for it is not just one color that grows from them, but many colors. Therefore, they are **composed** of so many colors—as many as proceed from them. Just as from the Earth proceed green, red, blue, black, etc., so also from the Water come all colors; likewise also from the Air and from the Heaven. Therefore they are **Collected Colors** <note>original Latin: *Colores Collecti*</note> made of all kinds of colors together...

<summary>This page explains that the four elements (Earth, Water, Air, and Heaven/Fire) do not possess a single "pure" color because they are the source of all possible colors. The author describes the elements as "Collected Colors," containing the potential for every hue that eventually manifests in the physical world.</summary>

# The Book /

**Of bodies.** <note>original: *Corporibus*</note> That is, it is not like a stone or wood or earth; neither is it like water, nor like the air. Rather, it is a **body** <term>original: *Corpus*; in this context, it refers to a distinct physical substance or entity</term> that has no mixture at all with the others. In the same way, the Heaven is also a body, which can best be understood through the image of smoke. For it must be so, for this reason: whatever grows from it is also of that nature. They are bodies and yet not bodies; they are **compact** and yet not compact; they are penetrable like water and yet not water; they are intangible like air, yet they are not the air. Thus is the Sun, which is a body as has been described, and the Moon and all the stars.

Now, it is without a **temperament** <term>original: *Complexion*; referring to the specific balance of qualities like heat or moisture</term>, and it is the element of fire, and a "Mother" out of which fire grows and springs forth. Just as fire has a **physicality** <term>original: *Corporalitet*</term> about it, so also does the Heaven; likewise the stars, which take their nature and essence from the Heaven. Therefore, know now concerning the Heaven, that this same body is a distinct part, and an element of fire; and whatever fire is meant to do, the Heaven does also—for the Heaven provides it and fire grows out of it.

Thus, know henceforth also of the color of the Heaven. The Earth has a color that is black through and through, and whatever other colors it possesses come from a **mineral source** <note>original German: *Miner*; referring to the fundamental source or "mine" of a quality</note>. Water also has a color through

### OF METEOROLOGICAL PHENOMENA <note>original Latin: *METEORORVM*</note>

and through which other colors exist, is also a **mineral source** <term>original German: *Miner*; referring to the fundamental matrix or "mine" from which a quality originates</term>. Now, the color of water has no name; it is not white, it is not gray, nor blue, nor green, and yet it may be counted among them all, or be regarded as such. The Earth also has a color; it is not black, it is not brown, etc., and yet it is something like them. So it is also with the Air, where one sees a pure color, transparent in the **chaos** <term>original: *Chaos*; in the natural philosophy of this period, this refers specifically to the atmosphere or the element of air as a medium</term>; it is not white, not brown, not yellow, etc., and yet it is something like these colors.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the various colors of the elements are blended together. The previous page ended with the thought that just as colors emerge from Earth and Water, so they also emerge from the other elements.</meta>

## The Book

...mixed together into one, and this mixture yields no [single] color; that is, it cannot produce colors with a distinct name that is understandable or known.

Now, consider the same regarding the Heavens and its fruits, through this example: You see what grows upon the Earth; those things have their tangible feet and roots in the Earth, such as trees and grass. But now, the stars are the fruits of the Heavens, and they belong to the Heavens. Yet they do not take root in the Heavens; they stand out beneath the Heavens without any support or attachment. This is the exact opposite of the Earth: one yields its fruit with roots, the other does not; one stands upright, the other hangs downward; one yields its fruit toward the heights, the other toward the depths.

It is the same in the water, as the fish provide an example; they stand upon nothing and have no feet, but instead swim in the water. In just the same way, the stars also swim in the Heavens, within the **body** <term>original: *Corpus*</term> of the Heavens. This is to be understood just as the water is the medium for fish; and they maintain the order of their swimming as God created them: high, in the distance, near, fast, slow, and so on. This I leave to **Astronomy** <note>original: *Astronomi*</note>; it is not necessary to mention it further here, except to understand that the Heaven is a body in which there is a swim[ming]...

# OF METEOROLOGICAL PHENOMENA

**B**

[Ele]ment is able to carry things just like water, and yet it is not water, but rather **dry**; and regarding the dryness that swims within it—which does not "swim" in the sense of being in a liquid, for it is not in water, yet it has "swum"—it has not walked or run, for there are no feet there, nor anything upon which they might tread. Rather, it is thus a miraculous work of God, and an element that contains all the others and encloses them together in a **roundness** <term>original: *Rotundiert*</term> and a circle.

Now, you should also have this understanding of the stars. They have grown out of the Heavens and stand within them in a flying manner, like a bird in the air, according to the order and the circle as God created them and as it pleased Him. However, they have grown only once and remain so forever and ever. The trees and fruits of the Earth perish and return again; the stars, however, perish only once and never return, and that is at the end of the world. Otherwise, everything that lies in the other elements is consumed by rust, moths, or death—except for the stars of the heavenly element, which remain. But their fruits come and go, such as rain, snow, etc. <note>The author distinguishes between the permanent "stars" and their temporary "fruits" or meteorological effects like precipitation.</note>

Now, they mostly have one color, which is fiery: Just as green is the most common color on the Earth...

shows and yet other colors more: just as the Sun, which is also uniquely special in its colors. For there is a distinction between fire and the Element of Heaven, as between *fire* <note>original: *Ignis*</note> and *Heaven* <note>original: *Cœlum*</note>. *Fire* is not an element, but *Heaven* is an element.

*Fire* <note>original: *Ignis*</note> is a *matter* <note>original: *Materia*</note> that cooks and prepares and brings things into the *ultimate matter* <note>original: *Ultimam Materiam*; in alchemical philosophy, this is the final, perfected state of a substance</note>; it is just like Death. Fire and Death are equal: fire consumes everything and takes it away, and Death does the same. Therefore, fire cannot be an element, but Death may well be: a visible death, a sensible death, whereas the other Death is invisible, which no one sees nor has seen. And just as *fire* <note>original: *Ignis*</note> takes away and entirely consumes, so too Death takes away man and all that has life. Thus, Death and *fire* shall stand equal in one degree. And the fire that *is* an element is the Heaven with its fruits, which are the stars.

For concerning fire you should know that it has been reckoned as an element, to be one of the four, which cannot be so. For Earth reveals itself as an element, and Water [reveals itself...]

# THE BOOK <note>original: *Das Buch*</note>

Therefore, observe now this number three; for every art that exists in Nature and is born from her should seek no more than three kinds of **species** <term>Species</term> <note>In this context, species refers to the distinct types or forms of matter that make up the physical world.</note>, just as the number in the **Godhead** <note>original: *Gottheit*</note> is neither more nor less, standing immovably in three, and can be neither less nor more. And any art that seeks more than this is false and errs in Nature, seeking within her that which is not there. These three are the **prime matter** <term>Materia prima</term> <note>the fundamental, unformed substance from which all things in the universe were originally created</note>; they have only one name, just as God does: thus, **prime matter**.

And just as in the Godhead there are three persons—one being the **Father**, another the **Son**, and another the **Holy Spirit**—so in this place, too, each species is distinct for itself, differing according to its **office** <term>Officio</term> <note>An "office" refers to the specific function, duty, or role assigned to a part of the natural or divine order.</note>. For there is the office of the Father, the office of the Son, and the office of the Holy Spirit <note>original: *officium Patris, officium Filij, officiũ Spir. Sancti*</note>. Likewise, there are also three kinds of offices in the **three primes** <note>original: *tribus Primis*; likely referring to the "Tria Prima" of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury which were central to alchemical theory</note>, which are nevertheless called by the single name of prime matter. And within such a number, it remains prime matter.

Furthermore, however, the prime matter was divided by God into four parts—that is, into the **four elements**—and they were brought into four beings, separated by God himself. Thus, whatever was of the **Earth** within the prime matter was separated into the Earth; [likewise] into the **Air**, into the **Water**, and into the **Heaven** <note>In older cosmology, "Heaven" often refers to the element of Fire or the celestial aether that makes up the stars.</note>. And each one was formed into its **predestined form** <note>original: *Praedestinirte form*</note>: the Earth as an Earth with its own office,

### THE BOOK <note>original: *Das Buch*</note>

The sun is fire, salt, and balsam. Snow, rain, and the like are contained in these three **Bodies** <note>original: *Corporibus*</note> within a single **Body** <term>Corpus</term> <note>In alchemical thought, a "body" is the physical manifestation of a substance.</note>.

I recount all this so that the study of **Meteors** <term>Meteorica</term> <note>original: *Meteorica*; in the 16th century, this referred to the study of all atmospheric phenomena, including rain, wind, and lightning, not just falling stars.</note> may be more easily understood; otherwise, it is well recounted and explained with greater understanding in **Philosophy** <term>Philosophia</term>. Now you should know that these first three—fire, salt, and balsam—may also be called by other names, as I mention in **Philosophy**: Fire as **Sulfur** <term>Sulphur</term>, Salt as salt, and Balsam as **Fluid** <term>Liquor</term> or **Mercury** <term>Mercurium</term>. These are the three that are called the **Prime Matter of things** <term>Prima materia rerum</term> <note>The fundamental substance from which all physical reality is composed.</note>.

### OF METEORS <note>original Latin: *METEORORVM*</note>

...[making it] more familiar to a physician and more understandable to a **Meteorologist** <note>original: *Meteorico*; a student of atmospheric phenomena.</note>. For just as the Earth is useful to man, the Heavens are just as useful to him. Every element is created for the sake of man; therefore, he should understand them well.

<margin>VB 3</margin>
Furthermore, it is necessary that we know concerning the elements: what an element is, and what it is not. First, understand this example: A human has a large body and various natures within that body; but that which truly constitutes the human is a soul and a spirit, and it is small. The reason we call the **Body** <term>Corpus</term> <note>In this context, the physical, tangible shell of a thing.</note> a "human" is because the human [spirit] is within the body, and the soul cannot exist without the body; rather, they exist together. The eye is large on a human; however, the power that sees is a small quantity compared to the eye itself. 

In the same way, the Earth is called an "element," though it is merely a bare **Body** <term>Corpus</term>; yet the **Element of Earth** <note>original: *Element Terræ*</note> is within that same body and is invisible—just as the spirit of man is invisible. Similarly, the Heavens that we see are a large **Body** <term>Corpus</term>; the element within them is a small spirit compared to the great size the heavens possess. A human speaks out of their mouth; it is not the body that speaks, but the spirit that speaks. Likewise, the Earth bears fruit; it is not the physical earth itself, but the element within it that gives the fruits.

<meta>This page continues the philosophical investigation into the nature of the elements. While the previous page ended by comparing the Earth's fruitfulness to the human spirit's speech, this page clarifies the distinction between the physical matter (the body) and the internal animating force (the spirit) within the four elements.</meta>

<header>The Book</header>
<page-num>4</page-num>

Thus, the four elements are **Corporeal** <term>physical or bodily</term>, but in their essence and nature, they are spirits. God is wondrous in His creations; He is not like a carpenter who builds a house out of material substance and cares nothing for the spirit. Such a body is without spirit; therefore, it yields no fruit, it is dead, and it is nowhere alive. For the spirit is living, and life is the spirit; and life and spirit work all things, yet they are one single thing and not two.

Therefore know that when it is said, "this comes from the element," you should understand it as coming from the element's spirit and not from its physical **Body** <term>Corpus</term>. The tongue speaks, and yet it speaks nothing; for it is the spirit within it that speaks, not the flesh itself. But through such mixing and union, the body is mistakenly thought to do all things and is itself regarded as the element, even though it is only a body and not the spirit. Just as flesh and blood do not make the human—rather, the spirit is the human—likewise, there are four souls or spirits: one is the Earth, the second is Water, the third is Air, and the fourth is the Heavens.

Now, understand further based on this: just as all things and fruits grow out of the Earth, and whatever grows out of her is also her <note>The text transitions here to the concept of the Earth as a motherly source of life.</note>

<insert>Mother</insert> <note>original: *Mutter*; catchword for the following page.</note>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the four elements are not merely physical matter but are animated by "spirits" or "souls" that provide life and function, much like the human spirit animates the body. He argues that the physical form of an element is just a vessel for its true, active spiritual essence.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Paracelsus’s explanation of the relationship between physical matter and the internal spirit. While the previous page established that the four elements are essentially spirits, this page uses specific examples—like boxwood, musk, and coral—to demonstrate how the true "fruit" or essence of a thing is its spirit, even when housed in a physical body.</meta>

# METEOROLOGY

<page-num>11</page-num>

Mother is the element of Earth: Namely, it has a **Body** <term>Corpus; the physical substance</term>, and within that body is the **Spirit** <term>Spiritum; the internal animating force</term>. The spirit is the true thing; the other is nothing. The Boxwood <note>original: *Buxus*</note> has a scent <note>original: *geschmack*, which can mean taste, scent, or essence</note>; that same scent is its spirit, and the rest is the body. Musk <note>original: *Bisem*</note> has its soul in its scent; if that is lost, it is like a cut-down, dead piece of wood that no longer gives fruit. Just as it is with scent—that is, the spirit is the scent—likewise, in Coral, the color is the spirit, and the rest is the body.

Thus, all fruits are like their element, possessing both a body and a spirit. It is not that which the eyes see that is the fruit; rather, the spirit alone is the fruit. However, there is a distinction between the natural spirit and the unnatural spirit. The **Natural Spirit** is physical <term>Corporalisch</term> and naturally embodied within a specific body. The **Unnatural Spirit** is entirely without a body. For although the redness of the coral is the coral itself, and the physical body is not, nevertheless the redness is a body—yet that body belongs so entirely to the spirit that nothing decayed or coarse remains within it. And because the spirit of the fruits is neither tangible nor visible, nature has given it a separate body in which to reside,

<sig>C iij</sig>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the true essence of any natural object—such as the scent of musk or the color of coral—is its internal spirit, while the physical matter is merely a vessel provided by nature.</summary>

# The Book

<page-num>11</page-num>

and to that same **Body** <term>Corpus; the physical substance</term> another **Body** from its **Element** <term>Element; one of the four fundamental building blocks of nature: Earth, Water, Air, or Fire</term>. For when it is said that **Corals** are good for something, it means their redness is good for it; because the redness is a pure **Body**, in which all power lies.

Understand also in this way concerning the heavens: the thunder that strikes—that is, the lightning-bolt <note>original: *Stral*, literally "beam" or "ray," used here to describe the physical discharge of a lightning strike</note>, etc.—is an essence drawn up and separated from all **Bodies** <term>Corporibus; the plural of physical substances</term> into its own body alone. And because the Heaven is the most subtle **Element**, and the only one that is the purest and most purged, therefore its **Spirit** <term>Spiritus; the active, invisible force within matter</term> has such great vehemence <note>original: *vehementia*; meaning intense force or power</note> over others, above all others.

I point all this out to you so that **Meteorology** <term>Meteorica; the study of atmospheric and celestial phenomena</term> may be thoroughly understood at its foundation. Through the examples of the other **Elements**, the **Element of Heaven** <term>Cœlum; the Latin term for the sky or the "Fifth Element"</term> may also be more easily understood. This understanding of **Meteorology** cannot be fundamentally grasped without the other elements; otherwise, one remains hung up in errors and confusion, which leads to a false understanding and interpretation of knowledge <term>erkantnuß; the deep recognition or realization of how nature works</term>.

The **Spirit** <note>original: "Geist"</note>, however, shall be in work continually, so that neither sleep nor the Sabbath makes it still and quiet. # METEOROLOGY #

...things, as has been said—and will be further explained—regarding the three first principles <note>Referring to the *Tria Prima* of Paracelsian alchemy: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note>, which are the bodily substance of all things. The Sun is a transparent and clear **Salt** <note>original Latin: "Sal perspicuum diaphanum"</note>, clarified, extracted, and cleansed <note>original: "mundiert"</note> from all darkness by these three first principles. And whatever darkness and **Shadows** <note>original Latin: "Tenebræ"</note> existed in that same **Mass** <note>original Latin: "Massa"</note>, those same **Shadows** were placed into a specific category; thus, that which was white and clear was put into one special **Mass**, and that which was dark was put into another. It is just as when one mixes white and black together: it becomes neither black nor white, but a color that is in no way **Perfect** <note>original Latin: "Perfect"</note> and instead becomes gray. But if they are separated from one another, then there is once again a good white and a good black.

In the pebble <note>original: "Kißlingstein"; likely a reference to quartz or silica stones.</note> there is a light, and in the flint as well, and in marble too; but the darkness and gypsum <note>original: "gibs"</note> [surround it] so that it does not burn, and thus no light at all is seen. But if it is struck out <note>As in striking a spark from a stone.</note>, then the light is separated and gives off its shine, and the stone remains dark. In this same way, the radiance was taken from the **Mass of the First Matter of the Heavenly Element** <note>original Latin: "Massa Primæ materiæ Elementi Cœli"</note>, and the white radiance was separated into the Sun, and the red radiance into the Moon and the stars. And in this manner, they were thus divided: the **Transparency** <note>original Latin: "Diaphinitet"</note> and **Clarity** <note>original Latin: "Perspicuitet"</note> of the white within the Sulfur <note>original: "Sul."; an abbreviation for Sulphur, the second of the three principles.</note>, in the Salt, and Li- <note>The text continues on the next page with the word "Liquore" (Liquor/Fluid), referring to Mercury.</note>

The **Gnomes** <term>elemental spirits of the earth</term> have all the skills of humans and demonstrate them too; the **Nymphs** <term>elemental spirits of the water</term> do as well, and so too the others. Yet there is no soul there for which Christ died, except for humans alone; and for those humans with whose blood and flesh He spoke.

Although all of this is no more **philosophical** than it is **meteorological** <note>original: *Philosophisch* and *Meteorisch*</note>, it is nevertheless necessary to mention this **philosophy** <term>Philosophey: here referring to the study of the fundamental nature of the world</term> here, so that in its **paragraphs** and **chapters** <note>original: *Paragraphis* and *Capitibus*</note> the **operations** <note>original: *Operationes*</note> may be **explained** <note>original: *declariert*</note> more thoroughly and without error. For much is attributed to Nature that does not actually come from Nature, but happens through such **Saganas** <term>elemental spirits</term>. This is clearly demonstrated in mountains, valleys, the earth, in the water, and elsewhere; for God is truly marvelous.

### ON ATMOSPHERIC PHENOMENA
<note>original Latin: *METEORORVM*</note>

...pher of the Air; the **Lemures** <term>here referring to spirits of the air, or Sylphs</term> know even more. <margin>On the philosophy of Man <note>original: *philosophis Hominis*</note></margin> Likewise, the **Higher Beings** <note>original: *Superi*; celestial or heavenly spirits</note> know more within the **Firmament** <note>the sphere of the stars and heavens</note> than Man does. Man only knows how to philosophize about what comes to him: namely, what is upon the earth, such as foliage and grass; what the **Minerals** <note>original: *Mineralia*</note> drive forth out of the water; and what the **Firmament** and **Air** <note>original: *Aer*</note> provide. That is where his philosophy begins. 

But the **Saganas** <term>elemental spirits</term> know what is in the **First Matter** <term>Prima materia; the raw, unformed fundamental substance of creation</term>. To philosophize and work within that primary state is a greater thing, to be more highly esteemed than the philosophy of the **Final Matter** <term>Ultima materia; the finished, physical form of a substance</term>. Although Man also understands **First Matter**, he is like one who watches a blacksmith forging from a great distance; the one who is actually inside the smithy is more certain of the work than the one standing outside.

<margin>Vulcan <note>original: *Vulcanus*; in this context, the transformative power of nature or the "inner alchemist" that completes creation</note></margin> By this, you shall be even better instructed as to what else is in the stars—that is, in the **Firmament**—which I have mentioned until now. Mark well what I present to you: **Vulcan**, who is the **Craftsman** <note>original: *Fabricator*</note> and workman of all things. He exists not only in the element of the Heavens—that is, in the **Firmament**—but he is just as present in the other elements as well.

### OF METEORS

...its seed into the **Ultimate Matter** <note>original: *Ultimam materiam*; the final, finished state of a substance</note>; that same spirit is **Vulcan** <note>original: *Vulcanus*; in Paracelsian thought, this is not a deity, but a name for the natural force or "inner smith" that processes raw material into finished products</note>. And although this is indeed a new **Philosophy**, that is of no importance. For **Aristotelian Philosophy** <note>the traditional academic system of the time, which Paracelsus famously challenged</note> is heavily filled with lies and errors, and composed of many masterly tricks and fables that can never be found within the **Light of Nature** <note>the true knowledge gained by observing the natural world directly</note>. Therefore, one should not believe a person on their word alone, for the Spirit blows where it wills—that is, not only in one person, but also in a second and a third.

Regarding this, know now about the **Vulcan of the Firmament**. He holds within his power the **Three Primes** <note>original: *3. Prima*; Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—the fundamental building blocks of all matter in alchemy</note> within the single **First Matter** <note>original: *Prima materia*; the raw, unformed substance of the universe</note>. From these, he creates in the **Firmament** <note>the sphere of the sky and stars</note> where he has his dwelling, everything that God has commanded him to make. He **composes, distributes, and orders** the rains, the snows, the frosts, the hail, the lightning-bolts, and all that are heavenly **operations** <note>original: *operationes*; natural processes or functions</note>. In these, he is the sculptor, the smith, and the preparer.

This **Vulcan** is not a spirit, nor is he a person; in this, he differs from the **Saganas** <note>original: *Saganis*; elemental spirits such as gnomes or nymphs</note>. Rather, he is a worker who does nothing more than attend to Nature, bringing out of her what is within her—that which God has placed in her—to bring it into the **Ultimate Matter**. It remains within the same: the <term>Archeus</term>, <term>Iliaster</term>, and **Vulcan** <note>These represent the inner "master of the house," the "primeval force," and the "natural craftsman" in Paracelsian philosophy.</note> draw it out and grant it from themselves. Now, however, it is no less true that not everything serves man directly, but rather through intermediaries. For the rain does not serve man without an intermediary, but rather through an intermediary—that is, foliage and grass, etc. <note>The author means rain feeds plants, which then feed or serve humans.</note> Now, understand the same regarding other **meteorological impressions** <note>original: *Meteorischen impressionibus*; atmospheric phenomena like lightning or storms that Paracelsus believed left "marks" or "influences" on the world.</note> alone. But there is another kind in the heavens; these serve man but are not "fruits" that are tangible, visible, or material; instead, they enter directly into man, just as the sun does with its light.

Although the sun demonstrates its operation most noticeably, sensibly, and visibly above all other stars, the other stars are of a similar nature; they demonstrate it just as the sun does, through their **rays** <note>original: *Radios*</note> into man, but unnoticeably, insensibly, and imperceptibly. Yet they are powerful, as they then prove themselves, and as is sufficiently reported in **Astronomy**.

# OF METEORS.

...to write. And first, about the origin and birth of the **winds**, and of the **predestined circles** <note>original: *Prædestinirten Zirckeln*; in this context, these are fixed, divinely ordained paths or zones in the atmosphere from which winds are destined to emerge.</note> of the winds. First, know this: the sphere and wind-circle consist of four parts—one toward the **sunrise** <note>the East</note>, one toward the **sunset** <note>the West</note>, one toward **midday** <note>the South</note>, and one toward **midnight** <note>the North</note>.

Now the circle is structured in this manner: just as the two elements, **earth** and **water**, lie in the middle of the **firmament**, and the element of **air** lies between the element of the heavens and the lower **globe** <note>original: *Globul*</note>. Just as the earth lies in the center and the heavens revolve around it, so too does a circle run horizontally across the middle of the heavens, earth, and water, surrounding them in a ring. 

As an example: take an **egg** and lay it on the ground—not from top to bottom <note>vertically</note>, but take the middle horizontally around the egg, so that the yolk is also in that same center, as the division itself shows. Now note this: just as the heavens revolve around the world like a circle, in that same circle are the **mothers** <note>original: *mutter*; the sources or "wombs" from which the winds are born.</note> and places of the winds where those winds arise that are **predestined**. And when they wish to go forth, they move from that same circle onto the **globe** through the element of **air**. And when they reach the **rotundity** <note>original: *Rotunditet*; the roundness or curvature of the earth.</note> of the world-spheres and

### THE BOOK

...strike [against them], so it may well be that the wind either strikes beneath the spheres <note>The text likely refers to the "globes" or shells of the elements—earth, water, air, and fire—as understood in the geocentric model</note>, toward those who are beneath us, or upon the spheres toward those who are upon them, or toward both parts, as it divides itself according to the height or the depth of the spheres. And thus the wind goes through the air over the sea and earth, as far and as long as there is any of it, until through long exercise and wide travel it consumes itself, is driven away, and vanishes, so that no more **matter** <term>Materia</term> <note>The physical substance that makes up the wind</note> is there.

<margin>Eurus <note>The East Wind</note></margin>
Now it is no less true that there are four parts, and each part has a special nature within itself. Namely, the part from the **East** <note>original: *Orient*</note> is warm and dry. Not that it receives this from the sun, that it should be warm and dry, or because it stands in the East—no, but rather because its nature is thus within the **three primes** <term>dreien Ersten</term> <note>The "Three Primes" (Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury) were the foundational components of all matter in the alchemical philosophy of Paracelsus</note>, to be so and not against its nature. Therefore, in the true **Eurus** <note>The East Wind</note> and its companions, no other nature and **influence** <term>operation</term> is found: they are all warm and dry.

<margin>Zephyrus <note>The West Wind</note></margin>
Conversely, the **Zephyrus** <note>The West Wind</note> at the setting of the sun is cold and moist. This is not because of the sunset, or because it stands there—for the setting of the sun is of the same **temperament** <term>Complexion</term> <note>The constitutional balance of qualities like hot, cold, moist, and dry</note> as the sunrise—but the nature of the wind's matter in the West is created cold and moist...

### ON METEORS <note>original: *METEORORVM*</note>

...to be. Conversely, from the **North** <note>original: *Mitternacht*, literally "midnight," the traditional German term for the North</note>, the creators of these winds are of a cold and dry nature. They thus create cold and dry **regions** <term>Regiones</term> wherever they are located. It is not that the winds take their nature from the regions, but rather that the regions are shaped by the winds, as the winds possess that specific nature. Likewise, in the **South** <note>original: *Mittag*, literally "midday"</note>, the **South Wind** <term>Auster</term> is warm and moist, the very opposite of the **North Wind** <term>Septentrio</term>. This is not because it travels over much sea or originates in moist places, but because its nature is simply such, and thus it produces its **effects** <term>Generationes</term> <note>literally "births" or "productions"</note> accordingly.

Therefore, understand that the winds do not acquire their nature from any external source, but possess it within themselves. If the South Wind were of a dry nature like the **North Wind** <term>Boreas</term>, no moisture would attach to it to make it wet; it would remain dry regardless of the sea, etc. Similarly, if the North Wind had been given a moist nature, it would be moist and not dry, no matter what the climate of the region was like.

Thus you should now know that the **origin** <note>original: *geberung*, birthing or generation</note> of the winds resides in the **Wind Circle** <term>Wind Circkel</term> and in their own nature. They are found within the stars, and the stars are their mothers. There are many of these stars on the **Northern** <term>Borealischen</term> side, the **Eastern** <term>Euroalischen</term>, the **Western** <term>Zephyrischen</term>, and the **Southern** <term>Australischen</term>—not just one, and not just three, but—

And as has been mentioned regarding the locations of the winds and their origin, from the furthest reaches of the Heavens, over the sea and earth, through the air: So know now, that these same stars have it within them to bring forth the winds according to their nature and property. In the same way, as you may understand from a tree, which gives its fruit from itself, out of its own nature, out of its wood, and out of its marrow <note>original: *Marck*, meaning the pith or inner core</note>: So it is also to be understood with such stars.

Now, the seed from which the winds are made is the **Prime Matter** <term>Prima materia</term> <note>the fundamental, unformed substance of all things</note> of the three Firsts, namely of **Sulfur**, **Salt**, and **Liquor** <note>original: *Sulphur, Sal, Liquor*. In the alchemical tradition of this period, these three principles (often with Mercury instead of Liquor) were believed to be the basic components of all material reality</note>. These three are the Mothers from which grow those things we call winds—the **Sulfur**, **Salt**, and **Liquor**. And in the **North** <note>original: *Mitternacht*, literally "midnight"</note> parts, they are of a cold and dry nature; those toward the **South** <note>original: *Mittag*, literally "midday"</note> are warm and moist; likewise also warm and dry are those in the **East** <note>original: *Euro*</note>, and cold and moist those in the **West** <note>original: *Zephyro*</note>. Just as the nature of the three Firsts is, so also is their fruit and produce—that is, such are those same winds.

### ON METEORS <note>original: *METEORORVM*</note>

Therefore, know that the winds have their growth within those same stars, following a common progression according to their season. Although [the wind is] sometimes less and sometimes more, they nevertheless rule in their appointed times when the winds reach maturity. In the moment of their coming-to-be, they receive their specific property and nature—just as the **Three Firsts** <note>The alchemical principles of Sulfur, Salt, and Liquor/Mercury mentioned on the previous page</note> are, so the winds remain. 

However, because they have such a long distance to travel, their powers become somewhat weak at certain times. For example, **Boreas** <note>the North Wind</note> cannot maintain his true character in the summer because the summer stars warm him. But in the winter he can, for he falls among the winter stars and remains unbroken. Even when winter is about to depart, he keeps his strength until he is finally tempered by the summer stars. 

Now, he [Boreas] is **Sulphuric**; therefore, he has the power to cause a "Sulphuric" disease. He is **Saline** <note>original: *Salisch*</note>; because of this, he splits the mouth open <note>likely referring to chapped lips or skin sores caused by cold, salty air</note> and takes on the property of salt-drops from cured meat. For this reason, he causes far more cases of **scabies** <term>itchy skin rashes or mange</term> and **alopecia** <term>hair loss</term>, and so on. The **Auster** <note>the South Wind</note> is likewise the same in his own season, at his best in his brilliance. Thus, each wind has its own time in the year in which it is specifically born and operates without being broken.

### THE BOOK

<margin>On the **origin** <note>original: *vrsprung*</note> of the **three [principles]**</margin>
Now, examine these earthly examples of the origin of the winds; namely, observe that when water boils in a pot, it releases a wind from itself. You can also see further that a wind is produced by all things that **ebulliate** <term>to boil, bubble up, or effervesce through heat</term>, whether they are dry or moist. Water that boils produces a warm, moist wind, as the process itself proves; a "dry boiling," as is known to the **Alchemists** <term>early practitioners of chemistry and metallurgy</term>, produces a dry wind.

Therefore, know based on this that the **Fiery Vulcan** <note>original: *Vulcanus Igneus*; in alchemy, this refers to the transformative power of fire personified as the Roman god of the forge</note> boils the **Three Firsts** <note>the fundamental alchemical principles of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury</note> within the stars, according to their nature. From this, the wind is born, along with its specific properties, just as the Fiery Vulcan acts upon wood. Indeed, the Fiery Vulcan is himself the wind, for he is placed within the Three Firsts. Where the Three Firsts are not present, there is no fire. He exists in the same way within the stars that govern the winds.

On this point, it is necessary to know that the Fiery Vulcan is nothing other than a **Sulfur**, a **Saltpeter** <note>original: *Salniter*</note>, and a **Mercury**. Now, when the spark of fire falls into these three, it enters into **Action** <note>active chemical or physical transformation</note> and performs its duty according to how the three are arranged. For the **Sulfur of wood** <note>original: *Sulphur ligni*</note> is different from the **Sulfur of the winds** <note>original: *Sulphur ventorum*</note>; the **Saltpeter of wood** is different from the **Saltpeter of the winds**; and the **Mercury of wood** is different from the **Mercury** [of the winds].

**Of the Winds:** Just as each type of wind exists, so too is its fire and its operation. For everything that grows does so out of those three. Therefore, it is first and foremost necessary to understand the **Three Firsts** <note>original: *tres Primas*; the fundamental alchemical principles of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury</note> well, for according to their nature, they are the mother of all things.

Now you see well what a common fire does in common **Sulfur**, **Salt**, and **Mercury** <note>original: *Sulphur, Sale, vnd Mercurio*</note>:
<margin><unclear>Hesse</unclear></margin>
How much more refined is this **First Matter** <term>the primitive, formless base of all matter</term> in the **Firmament** <note>the heavens or the sky</note>—stronger and more powerful. You should note this: that there is no other origin of the winds than when the Three Firsts are moved by **Vulcan** <note>the personification of transformative alchemical fire</note> in their operation; thus the **Three Firsts** <note>original: *Tres Primæ*</note> stir themselves into **Action**. This action then produces the wind and its nature along with it—whether cold or warm, dry or moist. As was mentioned above, just as a wind comes forth from a fire which it creates itself, so it is also to be understood here. For God has thus composed the generation of the firmament and given it such properties that the three [principles] should provide and bring forth all things in their places, wherever God has ordained them; and through their **operations** <note>original: *operationibus*</note> they proceed into the **Center** <note>original: *Centrum*</note> of the world.

Although **Vulcan** <note>In the context of Renaissance natural philosophy, Vulcan represents the elemental power of fire and heat, often associated with alchemy and subterranean transformation</note> is also in the earth / and in the water / he is not **lightning-like** <term>fulgurisch; from the Latin *fulgur*, meaning lightning</term> in those places: only in the firmament is he lightning-like and possesses a **stormy impulse** <term>Tempestuofischen Impetum; a violent, storm-driven force</term>.

Thus it is, that like a **gunpowder** <note>original: *Büxenpuluer*</note> [composed of] **Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury** <note>The *Tria Prima*, or three primary alchemical principles</note>, nothing can withstand it. And although it is not fire, does not smoke, and does not explode, it is nonetheless an **ethereal gunpowder** <term>Etherisch Büxenpuluer; a celestial or spiritual version of physical gunpowder</term> that comes from the **caverns** <term>Cauernis; from the Latin *cavernae*, meaning hollow spaces</term> and **outlets** <term>Emunctorijs; from the Latin *emunctoria*, referring to channels through which waste or fluids are expelled</term> of the **floodgates** <term>Cataracten; from the Latin *cataractae*, meaning sluices or downpours</term> of the **Vulcanic wind-stars** <note>Stars associated with the elemental fire of Vulcan that are believed to generate wind</note>. And it is thus a **wind-like saltpeter** <term>Windischer Salpeter; saltpeter was the explosive component of gunpowder; here it refers to a volatile substance in the atmosphere</term>, [along with] **Sulfur** and **Mercury**, which accomplish all things miraculously; and without these **three species** it is impossible for anything to happen—with the only difference being that one is according to earthly nature, and the other according to ethereal type and property.

# OF METEOROLOGICAL PHENOMENA <note>original: *METEORORVM*</note>

[The author] presents an example so that the visible things here among us and the invisible things above us may be understood in the same way. For it should be as it is said: that an extraction from other water, thus **ascending** <term>Ascendieren; the upward movement of vapors</term> and **distilling** <term>Distillieren; the process of condensation and falling as liquid</term> up and down, cannot be. The reason for this has been sufficiently indicated in the previously mentioned sections. Rather, we should know that every rain comes from its own origin and is born anew, having never existed before—just as a spring might have emerged this year which in previous years had never been there. So also are the seeds, where one follows the other and yields its fruit in its proper time, just as God created it.

Now, the number of the rain stars cannot be written down, for the entire **firmament** <note>The sphere of the heavens</note> is full of them, distributed throughout all lands as far as the **globe** <note>original: *Globul*</note> extends. And although it happens that in some **regions** <note>original: *Regionibus*</note> the **effect** <note>original: *effectus*</note> of the rain stars does not always manifest, but instead it rains seldom or not at all: this is not due to a defect, as if the stars were not present, but rather they are dried out by the winds and **constellations** <note>The relative positions of stars believed to influence earthly events</note> of that same land and region. Just as a tree may be dried out, or a piece of fruit that cannot ripen because of drought: so it also happens with rain stars, in those...

# OF ATMOSPHERIC PHENOMENA.

When a cloud emerges from the stars, it settles downward toward the earth into the air, where it hovers. Now, in itself, it is water, but not yet fully prepared. And its **matter** <term>Materia</term> consists of **Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury** <term>Sal, Sul., Merc.; the "Tria Prima," or three fundamental principles of all substances in Paracelsian alchemy</term>. These three, once outside of the stars, undergo **dissolution** <note>original: *Resoluierung*</note>, just like **tartar** <term>Weinstein; potassium bitartrate crystals that form in wine barrels</term> in a cellar or in water. Thus, the air is the "cellar" for this **calcination** <term>Calcination; here, the chemical reduction of a substance</term> or **digestion** <term>Digestion; the process of ripening or breaking down a substance through gentle heat or time</term>, where they **dissolve** <note>original: *Soluieren*</note> and release themselves.

So, when the cloud stands in the air as if in a cellar: if it is well-prepared for **dissolution** <note>original: *Solution*</note>, it passes all the more quickly; if not, it remains in the air longer. Sometimes, if there is only a small amount, it vanishes entirely, or produces a damp mist <note>original: *ein Regen der feuchte*</note> that is unable to reach the ground. But if it remains and is destined for rain, it then **resolves** <note>original: *resoluiert*</note> itself into water. And as the rain is, so is the **dissolution**; the rain comes from the clouds, and the clouds come from the stars.

Thus it often happens that such rain continues on and on, because the cloud-mass daily emerges from the **rain-stars** <term>Regensterne; specific stars or constellations believed to govern the release of moisture</term> and does not cease to increase—either one star follows another, or many work together, according to their **concordance** <term>Concordantz; the harmonious alignment or shared influence of celestial bodies</term>. For some stars give for an hour, some more, some a day, some many days, some a week, some many weeks.

<summary>This page explains that clouds are an intermediate, alchemical state of water composed of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury. It describes the atmosphere as a "cellar" where these celestial clouds "ripen" and dissolve into liquid rain based on the alignment of specific stars.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, clouds, rain-stars, Tria Prima, Salt Sulfur Mercury, dissolution, digestion, concordance, meteorology</keywords>

...weeks, some quite inconsistently mixed together, at varying hours, etc. However all that may be: first, the **Preparation** <term>Preparation; the initial alchemical stage of processing a substance</term> occurs in the stars from the Three Primaries <term>The "Tria Prima" of Paracelsian alchemy: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</term> according to such a nature. After that, the cloud-mass is formed. After that comes the **dissolution** <note>original: *resoluierung*</note> in the air, by means of the air. From that very thing, rain falls upon the earth.

    Now, the **dissolving** happens in this way: as soon as the rain-cloud enters the air—as if entering its cellar—and its time of **dissolution** <note>original: *Resolution*</note> has arrived, it then **dissolves** itself drop by drop, separating from itself. It does not fall in a single mass, but just as if it were **distilling** downward. This has been alchemically arranged by **Divine Providence** <note>original: *Diuina prouidentia*</note> so that one drop follows another, **filtering**, **distilling**, and **dripping** <note>original: *viltrire, distilliere, guttier*</note> in the manner of drops, just as they then come down to us. Thus it often happens that the **dissolution** occurs slowly, and therefore the drops fall slowly; sometimes they fall faster and more nimbly, according to the arrangement and providence—whether many or few, large or small drops, depending on the year and the season.

Thus understand the origin / of the natural and useful rains of the Earth / and for mankind / that they (as has been mentioned) take their origin naturally / and are thus ordained and created in their **predestination** <note>original: "prædestination"; referring to the divinely appointed purpose or natural destiny of a substance</note> / to moisten the Earth. Now there are other winds besides / and other clouds / which are not of rain / but of a dry nature: How these are to be understood / follows hereafter. What more is necessary to know concerning the stars / is learned in **Astronomy** <note>original: "Astronomia"</note> / for which reason I have omitted it here in this **Meteorology** <term>Meteorology; here referring to the "Meteorica," a specific branch of natural philosophy dealing with atmospheric phenomena</term> <note>original: "Meteorica"</note>.

### OF ATMOSPHERIC PHENOMENA. <note>original: "METEORORVM"</note>

Now regarding the dry clouds, know that there are also certain stars that are not inclined toward rain or rain-winds, and are not of the same kind as the wind-stars, though they are quite similar in nature. However, these are particular wind-stars, not universal ones. 

Just as with the wind-stars of the *Panelodis* <term>Panelodis; a specific Paracelsian classification for dry, luminous atmospheric phenomena or "meteors" that do not produce rain</term>, these wind-stars are beautiful in color, pure, clear, and high. The ethereal cellar <note>the "Etherische Keller" refers to the upper regions of the atmosphere, envisioned as a laboratory or storage vault where vapors are processed</note> cannot dissolve <note>original: "soluieren"; to break down or condense into liquid</note> them, for this reason: they are as dry as seasoned wood, which can never become water. Their nature is beautiful, subtle, light, and pleasant; they originate from a dry <note>original: "sicco"</note> **Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury** <term>Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury; the "Tria Prima" or three primary principles which the author believes constitute all physical matter</term> in which there is no moisture; therefore, they cannot be turned into dampness. 

Furthermore, these same winds are mild, pleasant, agreeable, healthy, and light for mankind. For they are well tempered, being composed of the good "Three Firsts" of the **first matter** <term>Prima materia; the fundamental, formless base substance of the universe</term> <note>original: "Prima materia"</note>. They gladden mankind and his blood; and whatever comes from them—that is, whatever comes from the stars—is well digested <note>original: "digeriert"; in alchemy, this refers to a process of slow, transformative heating</note> and clearly refined <note>original: "subtiliert"</note>. 

But where coarse species of the "Three Firsts" are found, such as in the rain-stars and the like, in those same places coarse clouds also form: black, dark, gloomy, and troublesome. ### ON METEORS

Just as a <term>spirit of coagulation</term> <note>original: "Spiritus coagulationis"; the active force that causes a liquid to thicken or solidify</note> exists in that place within the fruits of the water, so it also exists in the <term>Glacial stars</term> <note>original: "Glaueischen Sternen"; stars that govern ice and freezing</note>. And when that same *spirit* arrives at its appointed time in the water, it turns it to ice—it coagulates it. But the reason it has no permanence is because that water is not a <term>body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> that is meant to be [permanently] coagulated; rather, it is meant to be dissolved. And its <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur"; the essential spiritual quality or "dye" of a substance</note> is worth nothing in that place.

But if the <term>matter of gems</term> <note>original: "materia gemmarum"</note> were present there and not yet solidified, it would soon be coagulated in that same place with a firm and lasting coagulation. It is indeed true that if its <term>exaltation</term> <note>original: "Exaltatiō"; the process of raising a substance to its highest power or purest state</note> were to remain in <term>existence</term> <note>original: "Esse"</note> forever and ever, the ice would never be undone; yet it would still not be permanent in the *tincture* of the crystalline kind.

The stars of the Glacial kind rule the winter—whether in the water, in the fruits, or in the earth; wherever something exists that may contain moisture, it is subject to <term>congealing</term> <note>original: "Congelation"</note>. The other snow-stars and such are not to be strictly reckoned as part of the harsh winter in that place. For snow is the winter's rain, and at that time it is more convenient and useful than rain; for which reason God created it in such a manner. Therefore, understand now regarding the Glacial stars, which create the true winter, that they possess the *spirit* of coagulation in al—

If, during the summer, the weather is **westerly and northerly** <note>original: "Zephyrisch Borealisch"; referring to the Zephyr (west) and Boreas (north) winds</note>, and the Moon aligns in that manner, and the stars become fertile during that time, they proceed to manifest their snow in the summer. # ON METEORS <note>original: "METEORORVM"</note>

In the same way, it often happens that [moisture] settles in the heights, where it cannot be so quickly **resolved** <term>resolved: from the alchemical 'resoluiert,' meaning to melt or dissolve from a solid/vapor state into liquid</term> because of the **cold cellar** <note>original: "kalthen Kellers"; a metaphorical description of the freezing temperatures found in the upper atmosphere</note>. So it also happens that **summer stars**—that is, the stars of summer rain—become active in the winter and break forth. These do not emerge unless the winter stars are at their weakest; then they fall down and produce summer rain. And although the **Glaveish stars** <note>original: "Glaueischen Sterne"; likely referring to stars associated with ice or sharp, crystalline frost</note> fall in, they cannot **coagulate** <term>coagulate: "coagulieren," the alchemical process of a liquid becoming solid; here, freezing</term> anything while falling, but only in the freezing upon the earth. 

For it is not as people commonly say, that rain is turned into and formed into snow by the winter. The reason is this: the **snow-stars** have their own particular **tree** <note>original: "baum"; in this context, the structural origin or "growth" point from which a specific meteorological phenomenon springs</note> from which they proceed; therefore, it is not as people think. Also, even if a freezing were to occur [to rain], nothing would come of it but **frozen water**, which is not snow. For snow comes forth from its **Mother** <note>original: "Mutter"; the Paracelsian concept of the 'Matrix,' the specific source or womb of a substance that determines its essential nature</note> as snow, and was never water. 

Thus, understand now the origin of the **irregularity** <note>original: "irreganges"; literally an "error-path" or deviation from the natural order</note> in the growth of the stars on both sides, in summer and in winter. But where such irregularities do not exist nor happen—

In that same place, winters are at their coldest and summers at their hottest; this I will set aside for now and leave to **Astronomy**.

Now there is yet another kind of **winter star**; these produce **hail** <note>original: "Grandines"</note> and **ice-gems** <note>original: "Gemmas"; likely referring to clear, crystalline ice or large, hard hailstones</note>. These stars have their own specific circle to follow; they are the kind of stars that produce their hail and ice-gems in summer and winter, throughout all four seasons of the year, regardless of the region's nature, the **constellation**, the moon, or the wind-stars. 

They do not arrive at the same time as the winter snows, nor with any of the rains mentioned in the preceding **chapter** <note>original: "Caput"</note>. For their **generation** <term>generation: the process of being brought into existence or "born" from the elements</term> cannot occur in mild or gentle weather, but only in harsh weather and rough times, whether in summer or winter.

For **Vulcan** <note>The Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this refers to the natural heat or transformative force that governs the forge of nature</note> cannot reconcile **Sulfur**, and **Nitre**, and **Mercury** into one without a great **impetus** <note>force or violent movement</note> and much bubbling and wind. During that time, cold and wicked weather sets in, for they emit a cold from themselves onto the earth, so that they may be well recognized by such cold, even though they are born both slowly and swiftly <note>This likely refers to a long preparation time followed by a rapid physical formation</note>.

So take also an example from this: When a cold **Sulfur** that is white, a cold saltpeter that is fatty, and a **Mercury** that is mild are taken together into a **mixture**, and these three are to enter into one **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> so that there is nothing else there but **unity** <note>original: "Vnitas"</note>, they are so **contrary** at the beginning that it requires a long time before they come together, with rushing, bubbling, thundering, and similar turbulence. It is the same in the heavens within such stars, for they are of a nature even greater and higher than the earthly ones; therefore, their essence and manifestation are also more turbulent. Now as they go into **perfection**, two types separate from each other: those that coagulate <term>coagulation: the process of a liquid becoming a solid or semi-solid state</term> into one, from which arise the **gems** <note>original: "Gemmæ"; refers to the hard, crystalline hailstones previously mentioned</note>; the other into the **resolved** <note>original: "reſolutum"; the substance that remains dissolved or broken down</note>, from which

...becomes a rain. And besides that, with both, there is a common wind, cold, and turbulence.

Thus the star casts its contents out into the **cellar of the air** <note>A metaphorical description of the lower atmosphere where weather forms</note>. In that place, one part **resolves** <term>resolution: the alchemical process of a solid dissolving into liquid; here, turning into rain</term> and falls to the earth as rain. The other part **coagulates** <term>coagulation: the process of a liquid thickening or hardening into a solid</term>, for it contains **coagulated Vulcan** <note>original: "Vulcanum coagulatum"; in Paracelsian thought, Vulcan represents the transformative fire or "inner smith" that shapes matter</note> within it. From this, hail is formed.

You should also know about small hailstones, that is, **hail** <note>original: "Grandinibus"</note>. These are formed in the same way, sometimes accompanied by rain, and sometimes not. ...stars. And they also have a special path distinct from other stars, revealing themselves in winter or in summer.

And although this is so, they also differ in this: that which is **coagulated** <term>coagulation: the alchemical process where a substance thickens or solidifies</term> has a summerly **force** <note>original: "impetum"</note>, for summer **storms** <note>original: "tempestates"</note> are wintry in nature. Their affairs are like **hermaphrodites** <note>original: "Hermophrodite"</note>, striking in both ways; at one moment they may be summer-like, and then winter-like:

And there, two **opposites** <note>original: "contrarij"</note> stand together in one **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note>, which cannot be any other way than solely in **hermaphrodites**. Thus, two **opposites** and workers are harmoniously joined together.

## ON THE THUNDERBOLT <note>original: "DE FVLGVRE"</note>
### Chapter VIII.

Now take note further of this activity of the **firmament** <term>firmament: the sphere of the heavens or the sky, considered in this period as a physical structure where celestial forces operate</term>, namely regarding the **thunderbolt** <note>original: "Stral"; literally a ray or beam, but here referring specifically to a bolt of lightning</note>, which is the greatest and strongest work of the firmament among all other **meteorological actions** <note>original: "actionibus Meteoricis"</note>. For if the firmament did not make itself known in this regard visibly and essentially, it would not be possible to believe that such a power could even be imagined to exist within the nature of the heavens. It is so very wonderful, beyond all understanding—yet not beyond Nature, but rather within Nature—and for man, it is a great thing to contemplate. From this, it is proven evidently that God has placed **Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury** <note>original: "Sul, Sal, Merc."; the Three Primes or basic building blocks of all matter in Paracelsian alchemy</note> into the firmament, from which all things are **materially** <note>original: "Materialiter"</note> born.

It argues that the thunderbolt is the most potent celestial phenomenon, demonstrating the material power of the firmament.</summary>

# [OF METEOROLOGICAL PHENOMENA] <note>original: "METEORORVM"</note>

Now, although this type also originates from **meteorological actions** <note>original: "Meteorischen actionibus"</note>, it is nevertheless distinct from all the others. And there are specific stars that are inclined toward this type—only "fire-stars," and counted among the "summer stars," but they are as much stronger as they manifest themselves. For some stars are of the first degree, others the second, the third, and so on, up to 32 degrees. Within these 32 degrees, the **lightning-stars** <note>original: "Stralsternen"; literally "ray-stars," referring to the stars that produce bolts</note> stand so highly graded in their **Three Primes** <note>original: "Primistribus"; referring to the Tria Prima: Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury</note> and **First Matter** <note>original: "Primæ materiæ"</note>.

It should also be noted that among them, there are those that go astray and falter in their operation, or are too premature, so that they sometimes even appear in winter. However, when they do arrive, such an **impetus** <term>impetus: a sudden, forceful energy or momentum</term> is present that they care nothing for the time, the moon, or the "wind-stars," and so on; it breaks through. And the signs of their arrival are great heats, which act as heralds. This heat comes solely from their **decoction** <term>decoction: in alchemy, a process of heating or "cooking" a substance to mature it or extract its essence</term> and preparation, whether fast or slow, as it is prepared and found in the **First Matter** <note>original: "Materia prima"</note> through **Vulcan** <note>original: "Vulcanum"; the personification of alchemical fire or the "inner smith" that crafts natural forms</note>.

In these lightning-like stars, the **Sulfur** <note>original: "Sul."</note> is in the highest degree, the **Saltpeter** <note>original: "Salpeter"</note> is at its highest richness, and the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> is in its highest **contrariety** <term>contrariety: a state of opposition, tension, or volatility between different forces</term>. For the Sulfur is the body, the **Saltpeter** <note>original: "Nitrum"</note> is

# The Book / 70

understand that which is highest in heaven through the earthly, namely in this way: Take an earthly **Sulfur** <note>original: "Sulphur"; the alchemical principle of fire and combustibility</note>, an earthly **Saltpeter** <note>original: "Salnitrum"; the principle of explosive force</note>, an earthly **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"; the principle of volatility</note>, and bring these three through art into one **Body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note>. Indeed, then, the cannons and gunpowder provide a sufficient example for you not to dismiss this.

So know now, that in the heavens the same is taken by the <margin>B B</margin> **Higher Powers** <note>original: "Superis"</note>, that is, the **Masters** of the ethereal: the ethereal Sulfur, the ethereal Saltpeter, the ethereal Mercury. These are put together in an **Aludel** <term>Aludel: a pear-shaped ceramic vessel used by alchemists for sublimation; here, it refers to the stars themselves as containers</term>—that is, in their stars—and there they prepare the components together, as has been mentioned. First, they <margin>MMB</margin> melt the Sulfur, then the Saltpeter within it with gentle heat, and finally the Mercury; thus it unites together, yet without movement or **force** <note>original: "impetu"</note>, and nothing leaves them except only the remaining **degree** <note>original: "gradus"</note> of heat, as was mentioned before.

Afterwards, when such preparation has occurred, the star pours it out into the outer **regions** <note>original: "regiones"</note>, that is, into the air, and it creates a cloud. At first, this cannot be easily seen because of the height, and also because the subtlety of its preparation makes it invisible until it descends. At that point, one thing falls upon another...

# ON METEORS

# ON METEORS <note>original: "METEORORVM," the Latin title for the study of atmospheric phenomena</note>

Sheet-lightning <note>original: "Himlizen," referring to the silent flash of lightning seen on the horizon</note> goes first, and the thunder follows after.
